I truly believe there is a coordinated effort at my office and my aunt's hospital, where people are dropping hints in emails and meetings that they are evaluating me for some secret program or investigation.\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are coordinating against you, does this belief feel directly tied to your deep feelings of worthlessness and depression, or does it feel like a separate, persistent suspicion that exists even when you aren't feeling particularly low?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that because I don't feel \"depressed\" or \"worthless\" in that way—I just feel very blank, flat, and empty inside. I haven't really thought about whether the suspicion is tied to my mood, so it's hard to tell if they are connected at all."

I honestly don't even realize I've done it until someone touches my arm or calls my name to get me moving again.\n\nDoctor: When you have these moments where you freeze and stare into space, do you feel like your mind has gone completely blank, or are you lost in a complex train of thought or internal experience?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not really aware of what is happening or what I'm thinking during those moments. I don't even realize that I have frozen until someone touches my arm or calls my name to snap me out of it. It just feels like a complete blank space in my day.\n\nDoctor: When you look back over the past few months, would you say your lack of motivation and 'blank' emotional state were present well before these suspicious thoughts about your colleagues began, or did both start to emerge around the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes, the blank emotional state and lack of motivation definitely started much earlier, about eight months ago when my workload and caregiving duties intensified. The suspicious thoughts about my colleagues and the hospital staff only began quite recently, within the last two months.\n\nDoctor: When you are in these 'blank' states or during your daily life, do you find that your facial expressions are less animated than they used to be, or that you have difficulty showing emotion even when you feel you should?\n\nPatient: Yes, that is exactly what is happening. A long-term client recently complained that I seemed emotionally \"flat\" and disengaged in our sessions. Even though I know I care deep down, I feel strangely blank and heavy on the inside, like I'm on autopilot and my facial expressions and emotional responsiveness just don't come through anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods of bizarre behavior, such as talking to yourself or acting in ways that seem completely out of character to others, or is your behavior generally appropriate aside from the episodes where you freeze?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I exhibit other behaviors like talking to myself, and my behavior otherwise feels completely normal to me. The freezing episodes in the hallway or copy room are really the only unusual thing others have pointed out, which happen without me even realizing it until someone touches my arm or calls my name. I don't have any other bizarre behaviors, but those freezing spells do deeply concern me.\n\nDoctor: In your daily routine, do you find it difficult to perform basic tasks like grooming, keeping your living space clean, or maintaining your personal hygiene compared to how you were before this started?"
  }
]

Yes, keeping up with daily chores has become very difficult for me lately. I find myself putting off basic tasks like buying groceries and keeping my living space clean, often just spending my evenings alone at home because I feel too heavy and lack the energy to get started. It is a major change from my usual routine, where I used to be so organized and active.

I just feel fundamentally shut down.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, and has this happened to you in the past, or is this a completely new experience for you?\n\nPatient: This is completely new for me, as I've always been very energetic and active, but this shut-down feeling has been building for about eight months since my caseload and caregiving duties intensified. The more recent feelings—like sensing that people at work and my aunt's hospital are secretly evaluating me—have only been happening for the last two months.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these thoughts about being evaluated by others are based on actual evidence, or do you feel that people are specifically targeting you or plotting against you in a way that feels unshakable?\n\nPatient: It feels incredibly real and hard to shake, even though my supervisor insists it's just routine feedback and paperwork delays. I truly believe there is a coordinated effort at my office and my aunt's hospital, where people are dropping hints in emails and meetings that they are evaluating me for some secret program or investigation.\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are coordinating against you, does this belief feel directly tied to your deep feelings of worthlessness and depression, or does it feel like a separate, persistent suspicion that exists even when you aren't feeling particularly low?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that because I don't feel \"depressed\" or \"worthless\" in that way—I just feel very blank, flat, and empty inside. I haven't really thought about whether the suspicion is tied to my mood, so it's hard to tell if they are connected at all.\n\nDoctor: Besides these suspicions, have you noticed any other unusual experiences, such as hearing voices when no one is around, seeing things others don't, or feeling like your thoughts are being broadcasted or controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. The freezing episodes in the hallway or copy room are really the only unusual thing others have pointed out, which happen without me even realizing it until someone touches my arm or calls my name. I don't have any other bizarre behaviors, but those freezing spells do deeply concern me.\n\nDoctor: In your daily routine, do you find it difficult to perform basic tasks like grooming, keeping your living space clean, or maintaining your personal hygiene compared to how you were before this started?\n\nPatient: Yes, keeping up with daily chores has become very difficult for me lately. It is a major change from my usual routine, where I used to be so organized and active.\n\nDoctor: During these periods where you find it difficult to maintain your hygiene or keep your home clean, do you find yourself acting in ways that seem odd or peculiar to others, such as adopting strange mannerisms or unusual posturing, or is your behavior otherwise unremarkable?\n\nPatient: Aside from the freezing episodes I mentioned, my behavior is otherwise unremarkable and I don't think I have any strange mannerisms or unusual posturing. To get us started, could you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm a social worker, but lately, I feel like my feelings and motivation have completely dried up. It’s getting much harder to connect with my clients or even with my own life. I just feel fundamentally shut down.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, and has this happened to you in the past, or is this a completely new experience for you?\n\nPatient: This is completely new for me, as I've always been very energetic and active, but this shut-down feeling has been building for about eight months since my caseload and caregiving duties intensified. The more recent feelings—like sensing that people at work and my aunt's hospital are secretly evaluating me—have only been happening for the last two months.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these thoughts about being evaluated by others are based on actual evidence, or do you feel that people are specifically targeting you or plotting against you in a way that feels unshakable?\n\nPatient: It feels incredibly real and hard to shake, even though my supervisor insists it's just routine feedback and paperwork delays. I truly believe there is a coordinated effort at my office and my aunt's hospital, where people are dropping hints in emails and meetings that they are evaluating me for some secret program or investigation.\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are coordinating against you, does this belief feel directly tied to your deep feelings of worthlessness and depression, or does it feel like a separate, persistent suspicion that exists even when you aren't feeling particularly low?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that because I don't feel \"depressed\" or \"worthless\" in that way—I just feel very blank, flat, and empty inside. I haven't really thought about whether the suspicion is tied to my mood, so it's hard to tell if they are connected at all.\n\nDoctor: Besides these suspicions, have you noticed any other unusual experiences, such as hearing voices when no one is around, seeing things others don't, or feeling like your thoughts are being broadcasted or controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything unusual, and I don't feel like my thoughts are being controlled or broadcasted.\n\nDoctor: Besides these suspicions, have you noticed any changes in your speech, such as having trouble staying on topic, or have you experienced any periods where your behavior seemed disorganized or erratic to those around you?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any issues with my speech or staying on topic, but others have noticed some unusual behavior. My coworkers have told me that I will suddenly freeze in the hallway or copy room and just stare into space for several minutes. I honestly don't even realize I've done it until someone touches my arm or calls my name to get me moving again.\n\nDoctor: When you have these moments where you freeze and stare into space, do you feel like your mind has gone completely blank, or are you lost in a complex train of thought or internal experience?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not really aware of what is happening or what I'm thinking during those moments. I don't even realize that I have frozen until someone touches my arm or calls my name to snap me out of it. It just feels like a complete blank space in my day.\n\nDoctor: When you look back over the past few months, would you say your lack of motivation and 'blank' emotional state were present well before these suspicious thoughts about your colleagues began, or did both start to emerge around the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes, the blank emotional state and lack of motivation definitely started much earlier, about eight months ago when my workload and caregiving duties intensified. The suspicious thoughts about my colleagues and the hospital staff only began quite recently, within the last two months.\n\nDoctor: When you are in these 'blank' states or during your daily life, do you find that your facial expressions are less animated than they used to be, or that you have difficulty showing emotion even when you feel you should?\n\nPatient: Yes, that is exactly what is happening. A long-term client recently complained that I seemed emotionally \"flat\" and disengaged in our sessions. Even though I know I care deep down, I feel strangely blank and heavy on the inside, like I'm on autopilot and my facial expressions and emotional responsiveness just don't come through anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods of bizarre behavior, such as talking to yourself or acting in ways that seem completely out of character to others, or is your behavior generally appropriate aside from the episodes where you freeze?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I exhibit other behaviors like talking to myself, and my behavior otherwise feels completely normal to me. The freezing episodes in the hallway or copy room are really the only unusual thing others have pointed out, which happen without me even realizing it until someone touches my arm or calls my name. I don't have any other bizarre behaviors, but those freezing spells do deeply concern me.\n\nDoctor: In your daily routine, do you find it difficult to perform basic tasks like grooming, keeping your living space clean, or maintaining your personal hygiene compared to how you were before this started?\n\nPatient: Yes, keeping up with daily chores has become very difficult for me lately. I find myself putting off basic tasks like buying groceries and keeping my living space clean, often just spending my evenings alone at home because I feel too heavy and lack the energy to get started. It is a major change from my usual routine, where I used to be so organized and active.\n\nDoctor: During these periods where you find it difficult to maintain your hygiene or keep your home clean, do you find yourself acting in ways that seem odd or peculiar to others, such as adopting strange mannerisms or unusual posturing, or is your behavior otherwise unremarkable?\n\nPatient: Aside from the freezing episodes I mentioned, my behavior is otherwise unremarkable and I don't think I have any strange mannerisms or unusual posturing. It's really just those moments where my coworkers tell me I suddenly stop in the middle of the hallway or copy room, staring into space for several minutes. I honestly don't realize I've done it until someone touches my arm or calls my name to get me moving again.\n\nDoctor: Since your feelings of being 'blank' and your lack of motivation started long before the suspicions, do you feel like you have lost interest in almost all your hobbies and social activities, or is your focus still primarily occupied by these concerns about being evaluated?\n\nPatient: Yes, I lost interest in my hobbies like yoga and art outings, and started pulling away from friends about eight months ago due to that heavy, blank feeling. The suspicions about being evaluated only started two months ago, so my social withdrawal was already happening well before those worries took hold. I just feel too exhausted to engage with any of it."

I honestly don't realize I've done it until someone touches my arm or calls my name to get me moving again.\n\nDoctor: Since your feelings of being 'blank' and your lack of motivation started long before the suspicions, do you feel like you have lost interest in almost all your hobbies and social activities, or is your focus still primarily occupied by these concerns about being evaluated?\n\nPatient: Yes, I lost interest in my hobbies like yoga and art outings, and started pulling away from friends about eight months ago due to that heavy, blank feeling. The suspicions about being evaluated only started two months ago, so my social withdrawal was already happening well before those worries took hold. I just feel too exhausted to engage with any of it.\n\nDoctor: When you are at home or alone, do you ever find yourself maintaining strange or fixed positions for long periods, or do you have any specific, repetitive rituals that you feel compelled to perform?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I'm not sure if I freeze like that when I'm completely alone since there's no one there to point it out, but my coworkers frequently notice me doing something very similar at work. They tell me I'll suddenly stop in the middle of the hallway or stand in the copy room holding a file, staring at nothing for several minutes, and I don't realize I've frozen until someone touches my arm or calls my name. Also, I don't have any repetitive rituals that I feel compelled to perform.

I honestly don't even realize I've done it until someone touches my arm or calls my name to get me moving again.\n\nDoctor: When you have these moments where you freeze and stare into space, do you feel like your mind has gone completely blank, or are you lost in a complex train of thought or internal experience?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not really aware of what is happening or what I'm thinking during those moments. I don't even realize that I have frozen until someone touches my arm or calls my name to snap me out of it. It just feels like a complete blank space in my day.\n\nDoctor: When you look back over the past few months, would you say your lack of motivation and 'blank' emotional state were present well before these suspicious thoughts about your colleagues began, or did both start to emerge around the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes, the blank emotional state and lack of motivation definitely started much earlier, about eight months ago when my workload and caregiving duties intensified. The suspicious thoughts about my colleagues and the hospital staff only began quite recently, within the last two months.\n\nDoctor: When you are in these 'blank' states or during your daily life, do you find that your facial expressions are less animated than they used to be, or that you have difficulty showing emotion even when you feel you should?\n\nPatient: Yes, that is exactly what is happening. A long-term client recently complained that I seemed emotionally \"flat\" and disengaged in our sessions. Even though I know I care deep down, I feel strangely blank and heavy on the inside, like I'm on autopilot and my facial expressions and emotional responsiveness just don't come through anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods of bizarre behavior, such as talking to yourself or acting in ways that seem completely out of character to others, or is your behavior generally appropriate aside from the episodes where you freeze?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I exhibit other behaviors like talking to myself, and my behavior otherwise feels completely normal to me. The freezing episodes in the hallway or copy room are really the only unusual thing others have pointed out, which happen without me even realizing it until someone touches my arm or calls my name. I don't have any other bizarre behaviors, but those freezing spells do deeply concern me.\n\nDoctor: In your daily routine, do you find it difficult to perform basic tasks like grooming, keeping your living space clean, or maintaining your personal hygiene compared to how you were before this started?\n\nPatient: Yes, keeping up with daily chores has become very difficult for me lately. I find myself putting off basic tasks like buying groceries and keeping my living space clean, often just spending my evenings alone at home because I feel too heavy and lack the energy to get started. It is a major change from my usual routine, where I used to be so organized and active.\n\nDoctor: During these periods where you find it difficult to maintain your hygiene or keep your home clean, do you find yourself acting in ways that seem odd or peculiar to others, such as adopting strange mannerisms or unusual posturing, or is your behavior otherwise unremarkable?\n\nPatient: Aside from the freezing episodes I mentioned, my behavior is otherwise unremarkable and I don't think I have any strange mannerisms or unusual posturing. It's really just those moments where my coworkers tell me I suddenly stop in the middle of the hallway or copy room, staring into space for several minutes. I honestly don't realize I've done it until someone touches my arm or calls my name to get me moving again.\n\nDoctor: Since your feelings of being 'blank' and your lack of motivation started long before the suspicions, do you feel like you have lost interest in almost all your hobbies and social activities, or is your focus still primarily occupied by these concerns about being evaluated?\n\nPatient: Yes, I lost interest in my hobbies like yoga and art outings, and started pulling away from friends about eight months ago due to that heavy, blank feeling. The suspicions about being evaluated only started two months ago, so my social withdrawal was already happening well before those worries took hold. I just feel too exhausted to engage with any of it.\n\nDoctor: When you are at home or alone, do you ever find yourself maintaining strange or fixed positions for long periods, or do you have any specific, repetitive rituals that you feel compelled to perform?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure if I freeze like that when I'm completely alone since there's no one there to point it out, but my coworkers frequently notice me doing something very similar at work. They tell me I'll suddenly stop in the middle of the hallway or stand in the copy room holding a file, staring at nothing for several minutes, and I don't realize I've frozen until someone touches my arm or calls my name. Also, I don't have any repetitive rituals that I feel compelled to perform.\n\nDoctor: When you are in these frozen states, do you ever find that your body remains in a fixed, rigid position where you could be moved or posed by someone else like a mannequin, or is it simply a period where you are standing still and staring?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don't think I am rigid or poseable like a mannequin; it is more that I just stand completely still and stare blankly, totally unaware of the passage of time. I simply freeze in place, like when I'm holding a file in the copy room, and I only snap out of it when someone touches my arm or calls my name.
